| 36 | The **Political Economy** box in full — traditional and modern forms, the double test, five concepts, two issues, **rational choice theory**, and Marxist political economy's **social formations** | p.143 | L25 |
| 37 | Sociology as the behaviour of men in an **associative process**, and institutions as **stabilized systems of expectations and actions** | p.143 | L26 |
| 38 | The six named sociologists with their dates, including **Bentley** and **Giddings** with the **early 1920s** voting studies | p.143 | L26 |
| 39 | Political sociology's transfer of **bureaucratic structure** analyses to four kinds of institution, the five-item inquiry into **value-orientations, attitudes, habits, beliefs and prejudices**, and the box's reverse limb, subsystem sentence and nine-item roster | pp.143-144 | L27 |
| 40 | Psychology's derivation of **social psychology**; the **thirties and forties** trigger for the **'authoritarian personality'** with **psycho-analytic theory**; **Lasswell (1902-78)**; and the box's clause that it **may also be used for influencing political behaviour of a group** | pp.144-145 | L28 |
| 41 | Philosophy's six-item agenda; the **transcendental method**; **Comte (1798-1857)**, **'positive philosophy'** and sociology as the **'mother social science'**; the dating of behaviouralism to the **1950s and 1960s** and of the revival to the **early 1970s**; the Political Philosophy box's value-pairs and its **philosophical and historical method**; and the conclusion's concessive chain about Aristotle and the nineteenth-century separation | pp.145-146 | L29, L30 |

## Chapter-title convention

✅ The chapter is headed **Interdisciplinary Perspective on Political Science**, which is also its running head on every recto in the range. ⚠️ The repository topic title, **Interdisciplinary Political Analysis**, is a cataloguing label and is used for the file and the lesson headings only. ❌ The repository title is nowhere attributed to the book.

## Evidence-typing convention

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| ✅ | the chapter's own statement, reproduced or closely reported, with a page anchor available |
| ⚠️ | this session's reading, inference, caution or reconstruction — ❌ not a claim of the source |
| 🔶 | canonical owner material that is **not** in the chapter |
| 📰 | a dated statement, kept at its date and ❌ not presented as the chapter's own |
| 🔑 | a high-yield point, flagged for revision rather than for provenance |
| ❌ | a prohibition, boundary or explicit denial |
| ➡️ | a routing instruction to another topic's or subject's owner |

⚠️ ⚠️ **On bold text.** ⚠️ Blockquotes and spans explicitly introduced as the chapter's wording are reproductions of the source, subject to the normalisations catalogued below. ⚠️ Bold is **also** used for ordinary emphasis, for headings within tables and for editorial labels; ❌ a bold span is **not**, by itself, a claim of verbatim quotation. ❌ No exhaustive identity is claimed for every bold token in this file.

## Dated-anchor control

📰 **One dated anchor is carried in this file, at Lesson 10 only.** 📰 National Education Policy 2020, Government of India, Ministry of Education, approved by the Union Cabinet on **29 July 2020**; Part II, Section **11**, headed **"Towards a More Holistic and Multidisciplinary Education"**, whose paragraph 11.1 invokes India's long tradition of holistic and multidisciplinary learning.

⚠️ It is used solely as a live policy instrument addressing the organisation of disciplines, which is the fact the chapter reports at PDF p.138. ❌ It is not evidence for any claim in the chapter. ❌ It is not presented as endorsing the chapter. ❌ No doctrine, lesson or practice item depends on it. ❌ No second current linkage appears anywhere in this file, and every other lesson records a truthful nil in its pre-teach checklist.

❌ **No other post-2009 substantive, statistical, statutory or policy fact is introduced anywhere in this file.** ⚠️ Exempted from that statement: bibliographic forms and examination years, which are bibliographic rather than substantive, and the single standard-form correction catalogued at normalisation 7.

## Subject boundary

| ✅ Belongs here | ➡️ Belongs elsewhere |
|---|---|
| ✅ What political analysis borrows from other disciplines, and the limits of borrowing | ➡️ Approaches as criteria of selection, behaviouralism, post-behaviouralism and the five models of political analysis — **Topic 08** |
| ✅ The empirical/normative distinction **as the engine of the outward turn** | ➡️ The refused equation, Weldon, Strauss and the full fact-value controversy — **Topic 08** |
| ✅ The legal approach **as one of the seven partial approaches** | ➡️ The legal approach as a way of studying politics — **Topic 08** |
| ✅ The structural-functional relay **as a transfer between disciplines** | ➡️ Structural-functional analysis as doctrine, with its conservative-bias criticism — **Topic 08** |
| ✅ Easton's political system **as a borrowed concept** | ➡️ The systems model with inputs, conversion, gatekeepers and stress — **Topic 08** |
| ✅ Base and superstructure **as a borrowed model** | ➡️ Historical materialism as doctrine — **Topic 04** |
| ✅ Elite theory **as a borrowed proposition** | ➡️ Competitive elitism and the elitist-pluralist debate — **Topics 22 and 23** |
| ✅ Schumpeter and Downs **as an instance of model transfer** | ➡️ The economic theory of democracy and its criticism — **Topics 22 and 23** |
| ✅ Political sociology's roster **as the chapter lists it** | ➡️ Power, authority and legitimacy — **Topic 15**; political ideologies — **Topic 02** |
| ✅ The state as the site at which demands are reconciled, **as the chapter frames it** | ➡️ The concept of the state, civil society and the nation — **Topic 10**; diverse perspectives on the state and the welfare state — **Topic 13** |
| ✅ The international sphere **as named in the conflicting-demands sentence** | ➡️ Sovereignty, globalisation and international order — **Topics 11, 12** and the International Relations owners |
| ✅ The methodological instruction to begin from **historical antecedents** | ➡️ Indian constitutional and governance detail — Polity and Governance; the historical record itself — the History owners |
| ✅ Charismatic leadership **as an object political psychology helps explain** | ➡️ Charismatic authority as a type of legitimate authority — **Topic 15** |
| ✅ Philosophy Optional PYQs as **separately labelled application anchors** | ❌ Any claim that this topic owns a GS or Philosophy Optional stem |
